### [1] [Did You Steal My Shot? Pioneering Camera Motion Plagiarism Detection in Generative Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22267)
*Chengguo Zhang,Ping Ping*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper addresses the challenge of detecting camera motion plagiarism in videos, particularly from generative models. The authors create the first benchmark for camera motion analysis with 11 motion styles and propose a motion representation combining optical flow with fluid dynamics-based vorticity cues. Their method achieves a 3.02× improvement over the strongest baseline.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Camera motion is a high-value form of intellectual property that reflects directorial intent and requires professional equipment. However, generative video models can imitate these motions with simple prompts. Existing similarity detection methods fail because they operate on visual content rather than motion, and their training data entangles camera motion with visual content. Additionally, traditional optical flow is insufficient to represent complex camera motions.

Method: The authors build the first benchmark for camera motion analysis, comprising a motion dataset with 11 motion styles and evaluation protocols. They propose a motion representation that augments optical flow with vorticity cues from fluid dynamics to better capture complex motions.

Result: The proposed detector achieves a 3.02× improvement in plagiarism detection over the strongest baseline and remains effective on generative videos.

Conclusion: This work extends copyright protection beyond static content to dynamic camera motion, enabling detection of motion plagiarism in generated videos.

Abstract: Camera motion often reflects directorial intent and requires professional equipment, making it a high value form of intellectual property. However, generative video models can imitate such high value camera motions with simple prompts, while existing similarity detection methods mainly operate on visual content and fail to capture deeper motion similarity. This is mainly because their training data entangles camera motion with visual content. Moreover, traditional optical flow is insufficient to represent complex camera motions. We therefore build the first benchmark for camera motion analysis, including a motion dataset with \textbf{11} motion styles and evaluation protocols. Furthermore, we propose a motion representation that augments optical flow with vorticity cues from fluid dynamics, thereby better capturing motions. Experiments show that our detector achieves a \textbf{3.02*} improvement in plagiarism detection over the strongest baseline and remains effective on generative videos. We believe our work extends copyright protection beyond static content to dynamic camera motion.

</details>


### [2] [LINGO: Latent Initialization and Gradient Optimization for Sparse-view X-ray Novel View Synthesis and CT Reconstruction with 3D Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22849)
*Lifeng Xing,Dequan Jin,Kunpeng Bu,Peigeng He,Shihui Ying*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes LINGO, a unified framework that combines latent mask-space initialization with dynamic gradient optimization to improve 3D Gaussian Splatting for sparse-view X-ray imaging applications (novel view synthesis and CT reconstruction). It addresses key challenges including noisy initialization, positional insensitivity, and weak gradients in low-density regions by using voxel-level 3D filters from X-ray masks and adaptive voxel scaling strategies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: In novel view synthesis and CT reconstruction with sparse-view X-ray imaging, insufficient angular coverage leads to structural ambiguity and accumulated noise. While integrating 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) with X-ray absorption physics shows promise, it suffers from three key problems: noisy initialization, positional insensitivity, and weak gradients in low-density regions.

Method: The LINGO framework combines two main strategies: (1) Latent mask-space initialization - constructing voxel-level 3D filters from X-ray masks to robustly suppress background noise and provide reliable geometric priors; (2) Dynamic gradient optimization - employing adaptive voxel scaling strategy and dynamically scaling loss to adjust spatial resolution and explicitly amplify gradients in low-density structures. The paper also introduces the Initialization Point Cloud Structural Deviation (IPSD) metric to evaluate initialization quality.

Result: For novel view synthesis, LINGO improves PSNR and SSIM by an average of 0.72 and 0.0039 respectively over baselines under identical sparse-view settings, achieving comparable reconstruction quality within 5k steps compared to state-of-the-art models requiring 30k iterations. For CT reconstruction, LINGO shows average PSNR and SSIM gains of 0.36 and 0.0134.

Conclusion: LINGO effectively addresses the challenges of 3DGS in sparse-view X-ray imaging by improving both training efficiency (5k vs 30k iterations) and reconstruction quality. The consistent improvements across both novel view synthesis and CT reconstruction tasks demonstrate the framework's effectiveness in accelerating training and enhancing reconstruction quality across different sparse-view imaging scenarios.

Abstract: In novel view synthesis and Computed Tomography (CT) reconstruction with sparse-view X-ray imaging, insufficient angular coverage leads to structural ambiguity and accumulated noise. Integrating 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) with X-ray absorption physics can achieve promising results, but it suffers from noisy initialization, positional insensitivity, and weak gradients in low-density regions. In this paper, we propose a unified Latent Initialization and Gradient Optimization (LINGO) framework to address these issues. LINGO combines latent mask-space initialization with dynamic gradient optimization to improve point cloud structural completeness while accelerating training. It constructs voxel-level 3D filters from X-ray masks to robustly suppress background noise and provide reliable geometric priors. By employing an adaptive voxel scaling strategy and dynamically scaling loss, LINGO can adjust spatial resolution and explicitly amplify gradients in low-density structures. To evaluate the quality of initialization, we introduce the Initialization Point Cloud Structural Deviation (IPSD) metric. Experiments on the X3D dataset indicate that for the novel view synthesis task, LINGO improves the Peak Signal-to-Noise Ratio (PSNR) and Structural Similarity Index (SSIM) by an average of 0.72 and 0.0039, respectively, over baselines under identical sparse-view settings, achieving comparable reconstruction quality within 5k steps to state-of-the-art models typically trained with 30k iterations. For the CT reconstruction task, LINGO also demonstrates consistent improvements, with average PSNR and SSIM gains of 0.36 and 0.0134. These results highlight LINGO's effectiveness in both accelerating training and enhancing reconstruction quality across different sparse-view imaging scenarios.

</details>


### [3] [Enabling Vision and Cross-Modal Learning for Multimodal Stroke Recurrence Prediction: An Interpretable Two-Step Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22271)
*Christian Gapp,Elias Tappeiner,Martin Welk,Karl Fritscher,Stephanie Mangesius,Constantin Eisenschink,Philipp Deisl,Michael Knoflach,Astrid E. Grams,Elke R. Gizewski,Rainer Schubert*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether self-supervised pretraining on 3D CTA scans can reduce modality imbalance and improve cross-modal integration for stroke recurrence prediction. Two multimodal neural networks are pretrained and fine-tuned using different freezing strategies, then compared against baseline and scratch-trained models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Multimodal stroke recurrence prediction requires integrating heterogeneous clinical and imaging data, but modality imbalance causes models to over-rely on dominant modalities and underutilize complementary information. The effects of self-supervised pretraining and selective parameter freezing on modality contributions and cross-modal behavior remain unexplored.

Method: Two multimodal neural networks were pretrained in a self-supervised manner on 3D CTA scans and subsequently fine-tuned using two distinct freezing strategies. Performance and modality utilization were compared against a prior baseline model and models trained entirely from scratch.

Result: Self-supervised pretraining enabled more effective utilization of the multimodal image-tabular dataset, outperforming both the prior baseline and all non-pretrained models. The best-performing Vision Transformer-based neural network successfully overcame unimodal collapse. Synergy analysis revealed significant interactions between vision and both gender and CHD, suggesting clinically relevant patterns for stroke recurrence.

Conclusion: Self-supervised pretraining and strategic fine-tuning support more balanced modality utilization and enable meaningful cross-modal interactions in multimodal stroke recurrence prediction models.

Abstract: Multimodal stroke recurrence prediction requires effective integration of heterogeneous clinical and imaging data, yet modality imbalance often causes models to over-rely on dominant modalities and underutilize complementary information. While self-supervised pretraining and selective parameter freezing are commonly employed to improve representation learning and fine-tuning stability, their effect on modality contributions and cross-modal behavior in multimodal medical models remains largely unexplored. In this work, we investigate whether image pretraining on 3D CTA scans reduces modality imbalance and improves cross-modal integration for stroke recurrence prediction, a clinically critical task we recently addressed. To this end, two multimodal neural networks are pretrained in a self-supervised manner and subsequently fine-tuned using two distinct freezing strategies. Their performance and modality utilization are compared against both the baseline model from our previous work and models trained entirely from scratch in this study. Our results demonstrate that self-supervised pretraining enables more effective utilization of the multimodal image-tabular dataset, outperforming both the prior baseline and all non-pretrained models. Notably, the best-performing Vision Transformer based neural network successfully overcomes unimodal collapse. Synergy analysis reveals significant interactions between vision and both gender and CHD, suggesting clinically relevant patterns for stroke recurrence. Overall, our findings demonstrate that self-supervised pretraining and strategic fine-tuning support more balanced modality utilization and enable meaningful cross-modal interactions. Code is publicly available at https://github.com/ChristianGappGit/SSL_Pretraining.

</details>


### [4] [ProxyBuild: Text-Guided Structured 3D Building Generation with Mesh-Anchored Procedural Proxies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23386)
*Xiang Tang,Ruotong Li,Xiaopeng Fan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Summary generation failed


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivation analysis unavailable

Method: Method extraction failed

Result: Result analysis unavailable

Conclusion: Conclusion extraction failed

Abstract: Text-guided 3D building generation holds tremendous application potential, yet existing generative models typically output inseparable single meshes or non-interactive rendered representations. While procedural modeling can generate editable buildings with hierarchical structures, rule authoring is laborious, and even with the aid of large language models (LLMs), it remains challenging to effectively solve procedural rules under geometric constraints. In this paper, we propose ProxyBuild, a novel hybrid framework for structured building generation. We introduce the Mesh-Anchored Procedural Proxy (MAPP) as a novel intermediate representation, which tightly anchors building components onto geometric shells, thereby decoupling the generation task into two phases: proxy prediction and proxy-to-asset instantiation. First, we construct a building dataset with MAPP annotations to train our designed face-edge bigraph encoder. By explicitly modeling the feature interactions of topological elements on heterogeneous mesh graphs, this encoder accurately infers the semantic roles of faces and edges. Subsequently, conditioned on textual styles and attribute parameters parsed by LLMs, we accomplish high-precision asset retrieval and assembly by integrating a spatial placement logic with hard constraints. Extensive experiments show that ProxyBuild not only significantly mitigates common issues in building generation such as over-smoothing, component collisions, and structural corruptions, but also accurately parses semantic-free shells from diverse sources. Outperforming prior baselines across various metrics, our method can robustly generate structurally clear, detail-rich, and post-editable 3D buildings from text, thereby providing a reliable and interactive content foundation for downstream applications such as virtual reality and digital twins.

</details>


### [5] [Moonworks Lunara: Modeling Artistic Intelligence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22272)
*Yan Wang,Yanzu Wang,Maitreyee Joshi,Samiha Sadeka,Partho Hassan,Reza Jarral,Sayeef Abdullah,Sabit Hassan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Moonworks Lunara is a text-to-image model implementing "Artistic Intelligence" - a framework balancing creative exploration with preservation of semantic/artistic structure. It uses a novel Diffusion Mixture Transformer architecture and iterative training algorithm with human-created art injection. It ranks first in Aesthetic Quality and overall in blind human evaluation, while maintaining sub-10B parameters and sub-10-second inference.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper introduces Artistic Intelligence as a new paradigm - exploration driven world realization that leaves space for creative possibility while preserving semantic, artistic, and compositional structure. The goal shifts from merely getting images "right" to deeply interpreting meaning and realizing it as imaginative, expressive worlds.

Method: Novel Diffusion Mixture Transformer architecture combined with a new training algorithm that iteratively evolves the data distribution through informative sample acquisition and targeted injection of human-created art.

Result: Benchmarked against 7 models (FLUX.2-Klein-4B, Qwen-Image 20B, GPT-Image-1-Mini): ranked 1st in Aesthetic Quality (8.473 vs 8.457 GPT-Image-1-mini), 2nd in Emotional Resonance, competitive in Content Integrity. Blind human evaluation also ranked Lunara 1st. Strong on CLIPScore and LAION Aesthetic Predictor. Competitive on GenEval against 16 models. Maintains sub-10B active-parameter footprint and sub-10-second inference latency.

Conclusion: Lunara represents a frontier in Artistic Intelligence, achieving state-of-the-art aesthetic quality while maintaining computational efficiency. It advances visual intelligence by shifting the paradigm from correctness to deep meaning interpretation and expressive world realization.

Abstract: We formulate \emph{Artistic Intelligence} as exploration driven world realization, leaving space for creative possibility while preserving the semantic, artistic, and compositional structure that must remain true. Moonworks Lunara, a text-to-image model, implements this framework with a novel Diffusion Mixture Transformer architecture. A new training algorithm iteratively evolves the data distribution through informative sample acquisition and targeted injection of human-created art. We benchmark Lunara against seven image-generation models, including FLUX.2-Klein-4B, Qwen-Image (20B), and GPT-Image-1-Mini. With GPT-5.6 Sol as evaluator, Lunara ranks first in \emph{Aesthetic Quality (8.473 vs. 8.457 GPT-Image-1-mini)}, second in \emph{Emotional Resonance}, and remains competitive in \emph{Content Integrity}. A blind human evaluation over the same evaluation set corroborates the automated metrics, ranking Lunara first. It also stays among the strongest models under conventional measures including CLIPScore and LAION Aesthetic Predictor. On GenEval, Lunara achieves competitive performance against a broader set of 16 models, including GPT Image 2 and Seedream 4.0. These results place Lunara at the frontier with Artistic Intelligence while maintaining a sub-10B active-parameter footprint and sub-10-second inference latency. Lunara advances the general visual intelligence frontier by shifting the question from whether models can get images right to how deeply they can interpret meaning and realize it as imaginative, expressive worlds.

</details>


### [6] [Mira-Scene: Pixel-Aligned Layouts for Generative 3D Scene](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23796)
*Yang-Tian Sun,Tianjia Liu,Zehuan Huang,Yi-Hua Huang,Xiaoyang Lyu,Ziyi Yang,Zi-Xin Zou,Yuan-Chen Guo,Yan-Pei Cao,Xiaojuan Qi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Mira-Scene is a compositional 3D scene reconstruction framework that uses dense, bounded correspondence recovery (Canonical Coordinate Map) to accurately place objects into coherent scene layouts, outperforming baselines with 39.8% relative gain in 3D-IoU.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Single-image 3D object generation struggles with accurate scene placement. Holistic methods sacrifice object detail, while compositional methods use sparse, unbounded pose variables that are difficult to learn and generalize poorly with limited supervision.

Method: Mira-Scene introduces the Canonical Coordinate Map (CCM), a pixel-aligned field mapping visible object pixels to bounded canonical space coordinates. Paired with a scene-space Point Cloud Map (PCM), CCM induces dense canonical-to-scene correspondences for robust geometric alignment. A multimodal diffusion transformer jointly generates object geometry and CCMs using modality-specific expert streams with shared attention.

Result: Experiments on indoor, outdoor, synthetic, and in-the-wild scenes show Mira-Scene substantially outperforms strong baselines, achieving 39.8% relative gain in 3D-IoU and 16.5% gain in 2D-IoU over SAM3D, using limited open-source training data.

Conclusion: Mira-Scene successfully replaces sparse pose regression with dense correspondence recovery, enabling training from scalable object-level 3D data without scene-level annotations while maintaining high object fidelity and accurate scene layout placement.

Abstract: Single-image 3D object generation can now produce high-fidelity assets, yet accurately placing them into a coherent scene layout remains an open challenge. A central difficulty lies in how object layout is represented. Holistic methods absorb placement into a scene-level generation process, sacrificing object-level detail. Compositional methods preserve object fidelity by decoupling geometry from layout, but typically parameterize layout as sparse, unbounded pose variables that are difficult to learn and generalize poorly under scarce scene-level supervision.We present Mira-Scene, a compositional 3D scene reconstruction framework that replaces sparse pose regression with dense, bounded correspondence recovery. At its core is the Canonical Coordinate Map (CCM), a pixel-aligned field that maps each visible object pixel to a surface coordinate in the object's bounded canonical space. When paired with a scene-space Point Cloud Map (PCM) from monocular geometry estimation, CCM induces dense canonical-to-scene correspondences from which object transformations are recovered through robust geometric alignment. Because CCM operates in bounded canonical space, it provides a stable prediction target that can be trained from scalable object-level 3D data without requiring scene-level layout annotations. Mira-Scene further introduces a multimodal diffusion transformer that jointly generates object geometry and CCMs, using modality-specific expert streams with shared attention and positional encoding to promote geometry-layout consistency. Experiments on indoor, outdoor, synthetic, and in-the-wild scenes show that Mira-Scene substantially outperforms strong baselines in layout accuracy, achieving relative gains of 39.8% in 3D-IoU and 16.5% in 2D-IoU over SAM3D, using limited open-source training data.

</details>


### [7] [Performance vs Consistency: Evaluating a Foundation Model in Lung-RADS Screening](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22281)
*Benjamin Renoust,Pierre Baudot,Tiffany Foriel,Yousra Haddou,Charles Voyton,Pierre-Henri Siot,Ezequiel Geremia,Danny Francis,Jean-Christophe Brisset,Valérie Bourdès,Sylvain Bodard,Benoit Huet*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This study evaluates MedGemma, a medical foundation model derived from Gemini, against 12 radiologists for lung cancer screening interpretation using Lung-RADS v2022 on the NLST dataset. Fine-tuned MedGemma achieved AUC 0.83 vs. radiologists' mean AUC 0.90, while the native model achieved only 0.70.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Foundation models show strong capabilities in medical imaging but their performance in structured clinical interpretation settings remains underexplored. Interpretative variability persists in lung cancer screening despite standardized frameworks like Lung-RADS.

Method: Multi-reader design with 12 radiologists independently evaluating each case from the NLST dataset. Compared native foundation model (MedGemma), fine-tuned version, and radiologists using Lung-RADS v2022 assessment.

Result: Radiologists achieved mean AUC of 0.90 with substantial inter-reader variability (range: 0.80-0.94). Native foundation model achieved AUC of 0.70 (below clinical relevance). Fine-tuning significantly improved performance to AUC 0.83, falling within the lower range of radiologist performance. The model produces deterministic outputs unlike the inter-reader variability among radiologists.

Conclusion: Fine-tuned foundation models can serve as complementary tools for clinical decision support, particularly in settings with limited expertise. However, evaluation on case-enriched cohort limits direct clinical generalization, and external validation is needed.

Abstract: Foundation models have recently demonstrated strong capabilities across a wide range of medical imaging tasks. However, their performance in structured clinical interpretation settings remains insufficiently explored. In lung cancer screening, interpretative variability persists despite standardized frameworks such as Lung-RADS. In this study, we evaluate MedGemma, a medical general-purpose foundation model derived from Gemini and its fine-tuned version adapted for lung cancer detection and diagnosis, compared against radiologists performing Lung-RADS v2022 assessment on the NLST dataset. Twelve radiologists independently evaluated each case in a multi-reader design, enabling quantification of inter-reader variability. Radiologists achieved a mean AUC of 0.90, with substantial variability across readers (range: 0.80-0.94). The native foundation model achieved an AUC of 0.70, failing to reach clinically relevant performance. In contrast, fine-tuning significantly improved performance to an AUC of 0.83, placing the model within the lower range of individual radiologists performance. These findings highlight a trade-off between peak accuracy and prediction consistency. Unlike radiologists, under fixed conditions, the model produces deterministic outputs, removing inter-run variability under identical inputs, in contrast to inter-reader variability observed among radiologists. This supports the role of fine-tuned foundation models potential complementary tools for clinical decision support, particularly in settings with limited expertise. However, evaluation is performed on a case-enriched cohort from NLST and does not account for real-world prevalence or external validation, limiting direct clinical generalization.

</details>


### [8] [VISTA: Video-Injected Stylized Text-to-Animation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23817)
*Monseej Purkayastha,Anindita Ghosh,Philipp Slusallek*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: VISTA is a two-stage framework that generates stylized 3D human motion by combining text prompts (content) with reference videos (style), without requiring jointly paired training data. It uses a Dual-channel Autoencoder for latent space mapping and a masked autoregressive diffusion backbone with Dual-AdaLN for style injection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper addresses the challenge of generating stylized 3D human motion that combines structural content from text with expressive style from reference videos, while avoiding the need for expensive jointly paired (text, video, stylized motion) triplets for training.

Method: 1) Dual-channel Autoencoder maps motion sequences and video clips into a shared latent manifold. 2) Masked autoregressive diffusion backbone operates in this manifold. 3) Dual-AdaLN pathway enables late fusion of video-derived style while preserving text-conditioned content. 4) Cross-batch unpaired training protocol with latent cycle consistency enables joint learning across separate datasets.

Result: VISTA achieves the highest style recognition accuracy among video-conditioned methods while preserving competitive content alignment. The 3-way classifier-free guidance provides independent user-controllable calibration of content-style balance at inference time.

Conclusion: The framework successfully demonstrates controllable animation synthesis with high style fidelity and content preservation, enabled by unpaired training and decomposed guidance control.

Abstract: We present VISTA, a two-stage framework for generating stylized 3D human motion by fusing structural content from text prompts with expressive style from reference videos, without requiring jointly paired (text, video, stylized motion) triplets. A Dual-channel Autoencoder first maps motion sequences and video clips into a shared latent manifold. A masked autoregressive diffusion backbone then operates within this manifold, injecting video-derived style through a dedicated late-fusion Dual-AdaLN pathway while preserving text-conditioned content structure. A cross-batch unpaired training protocol with latent cycle consistency enables joint learning across separate semantically rich and stylistically diverse datasets. As a proof-of-concept for controllable animation synthesis, we validate VISTA on rendered motion-capture references: it achieves the highest style recognition accuracy among video-conditioned methods while preserving competitive content alignment, and its decomposed 3-way classifier-free guidance provides independent, user-controllable calibration of the content--style balance at inference time.

</details>


### [9] [Brain-to-Image Generation: Reconstructing Visual Stimuli from EEG using Generative Adversarial Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22282)
*Harshit Goyal*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper presents a baseline method for retrieving viewed images from EEG signals in a visual embedding space, rather than direct pixel reconstruction. Using a temporal-spatial convolutional encoder on THINGS-EEG2 dataset, the model maps EEG features to pretrained ViT-B/32 image features, achieving 12.83%, 39.17%, and 58.00% recall at top-1, 5, and 10 respectively (well above 0.5%, 2.5%, 5.0% chance levels).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is to address the challenging problem of reconstructing visual stimuli from EEG, which has high temporal but limited spatial resolution. The authors argue that testing whether EEG can retrieve stimuli in a visual embedding space is more defensible than direct pixel reconstruction, given the limitations of scalp EEG and small dataset sizes.

Method: The method uses a compact temporal-spatial convolutional encoder that maps repetition-averaged EEG (63 channels × 250 time points) to 512-dimensional ViT-B/32 image features extracted from a pretrained vision transformer. Model selection uses a concept-disjoint validation split, and final evaluation uses the official 200-image, 200-concept test gallery. Experiments include cross-subject generalization and ablation studies on test repetitions.

Result: The model achieves 12.83% ± 0.58%, 39.17% ± 1.76%, and 58.00% ± 1.73% image recall at top-1, 5, and 10 (mean ± std), compared to analytical chance levels of 0.5%, 2.5%, and 5.0%. Session-balanced ablation shows averaging more test repetitions improves ranking. Cross-subject application causes sharp performance drop, indicating subject specificity. Direct conditional generators without pretrained visual weights fail, producing noise-dominated outputs.

Conclusion: The results support above-chance coarse semantic decoding under a closed-set, repetition-averaged protocol, but do not support faithful recovery of stimulus pixels. The work provides a reproducible single-subject baseline for EEG-based image retrieval in semantic embedding space.

Abstract: Reconstructing visual stimuli from electroencephalography (EEG) is difficult because scalp measurements have high temporal but limited spatial resolution, and paired EEG-image datasets remain small relative to modern generative-model training corpora. We present a reproducible single-subject baseline on THINGS-EEG2 that first tests the more defensible question of whether EEG can retrieve the viewed stimulus in a visual embedding space. A compact temporal-spatial convolutional encoder maps repetition-averaged EEG (63 by 250) to provided 512-dimensional ViT-B/32 image features. Model selection uses a concept-disjoint validation split, and final evaluation uses the official 200-image, 200-concept test gallery. Across three training seeds, the model obtains 12.83 +/- 0.58%, 39.17 +/- 1.76%, and 58.00 +/- 1.73% image recall at 1, 5, and 10 (mean +/- sample standard deviation), compared with analytical chance levels of 0.5%, 2.5%, and 5.0%. A session-balanced ablation shows that averaging more test repetitions generally improves ranking. Applying the Subject 01 model to the other nine subjects without adaptation causes a sharp performance drop, exposing subject specificity. We further report exploratory stress tests of direct conditional generators trained without external visual weights: single-subject and ten-subject variants produce noise-dominated outputs, with early validation improvements reversing after one to four epochs. Finally, we distinguish direct reconstruction from semantic rendering with a pretrained diffusion prior. The results support above-chance coarse semantic decoding under a closed-set, repetition-averaged protocol, but do not support faithful recovery of stimulus pixels.

</details>


### [10] [Estimating Accurate Hand Pose in Camera Space with Vision Transformer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24424)
*Kaiwen Ren,Yiran Jiang,Yongjing Ye,Shihong Xia*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A monocular RGB hand pose estimation framework that addresses depth ambiguity and the coupling effect between local hand poses and global wrist positions through two novel techniques: Transformation-Isomorphism Supervision and Perspective Information Embedding, integrated within an encoder-decoder architecture with a framerate-aware multi-dataset training strategy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Monocular RGB-based hand pose estimation faces two fundamental challenges: (1) depth ambiguity inherent to monocular settings, and (2) the coupling effect where hand projections are jointly determined by local poses, wrist positions, and camera intrinsics, making it difficult to disentangle local and global pose estimation.

Method: The framework proposes two key innovations: (1) Transformation-Isomorphism Supervision for extracting hand-depth information, and (2) Perspective Information Embedding for resolving the coupling effect between local pose and wrist position. Both are integrated into a mainstream encoder-decoder architecture. Additionally, a novel framerate-aware multi-dataset training strategy is proposed for sequential pose refinement.

Result: The approach achieves 37.1% improvement in CS-MJE (Common Sense Mean Joint Error) over the state-of-the-art on the HO3D dataset.

Conclusion: The fully integrated approach successfully addresses the depth ambiguity and coupling challenges in monocular RGB hand pose estimation, demonstrating significant performance gains over existing methods.

Abstract: Monocular RGB-based hand pose estimation has emerged as a critical research frontier in computer vision. The local hand pose estimation methods predict hand poses relative to the wrist, while global hand pose estimation also requires estimating the wrist's position in the camera coordinate system. However, this camera-space estimation confronts two fundamental challenges: (1) depth ambiguity in monocular settings, and (2) the coupling effect of hand local poses and global wrist positions in the perspective projections. In particular, this coupling reflects that the projections are jointly determined by local hand poses, wrist positions, and camera intrinsics. To overcome these challenges, our framework proposes two key innovations: Transformation-Isomorphism Supervision for hand-depth information extraction and Perspective Information Embedding for resolving above coupling effect of local pose and wrist position, both integrated within the mainstream encoder-decoder architecture. Besides, we propose a novel framerate-aware multi-dataset training strategy for sequential pose refinement. Our fully integrated approach achieves at most 37.1\% superiority in CS-MJE over SOTA on HO3D. Project page: https://github.com/Mine268/CS-ViT.

</details>


### [11] [Rethinking Streaming Video Diffusion Model: Context, Execution, and Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22283)
*Hongchen Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper develops a unified analytical framework for streaming video diffusion that explores the design space of model choices, sampler choices, historical conditioning, execution scheduling, and training strategies. The framework accommodates various causal context-selection policies and makes their computational dependencies explicit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding the design space of streaming video diffusion is essential to explore its potential for generation quality and computational efficiency. The research aims to break the assumption that fully denoised history is a prerequisite for high-quality streaming generation.

Method: The authors developed a unified analytical framework that relates model and sampler choices, historical conditioning, execution scheduling, and training strategies. They studied three representative policies: clean (full denoised history), same-level, and progressive history. The framework was evaluated on the full VBench prompt set, and long-video comparisons were conducted. They also explored LoRA adaptation of the DMD fake-score network.

Result: On VBench: Same-level achieved 85.24, progressive history achieved 85.60, compared to clean-history reference at 84.45. Progressive history showed improved subject consistency and more coherent motion in long videos. Progressive-history pipelining achieved 1.57-2.83x steady-state DiT speedups. LoRA adaptation improved generation quality using only 2.15% of trainable parameters compared to full-parameter adaptation.

Conclusion: Fully denoised history is not a prerequisite for high-quality streaming generation. The findings motivate the joint design of historical conditioning, execution, and training strategies for optimal results.

Abstract: Understanding the design space of streaming video diffusion is essential to exploring its potential for generation quality and computational efficiency. We develop a unified analytical framework that relates model and sampler choices, historical conditioning, execution scheduling, and training strategies. The framework accommodates a broad family of causal context-selection policies and makes their computational dependencies and training-inference alignment explicit. Within this design space, we study three representative policies: clean, same-level, and progressive history. On the full VBench prompt set, same-level and progressive history achieve aggregate scores of 85.24 and 85.60, respectively, compared with 84.45 for the clean-history reference. Long-video comparisons further show improved subject consistency and more coherent motion with progressive history. By allowing multiple denoising nodes to be processed together, progressive-history pipelining achieves $1.57$-$2.83\times$ steady-state DiT speedups under our evaluated conditions. We additionally find that LoRA adaptation of the DMD fake-score network improves generation quality using only 2.15% as many trainable fake-score parameters as full-parameter adaptation. Together, these findings show that fully denoised history is not a prerequisite for high-quality streaming generation and motivate the joint design of historical conditioning, execution, and training.

</details>


### [12] [Style as Cover: Deep Image Steganography via Stylized Transmission](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22392)
*Qi Li,Jidong Yang,Huaike Yu,Chunpeng Wang,Suo Gao,Herbert Ho-Ching Iu,Yuantian Miao,Bin Ma,Xiao Chen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: StyleStegaNet proposes a novel stylized image hiding framework that redefines steganography invisibility from cover-preserving concealment to behavior-level camouflage using style transformation. It uses a four-stage approach with progressive training to address challenges in secret recovery when neural stylization alters feature statistics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Traditional cover-preserving steganography becomes vulnerable when original covers are exposed or can be approximated. This paper aims to address this vulnerability by proposing style-concealment transmission, where stylized stego images are generated conditioned on publicly available style references, making detection extremely difficult.

Method: StyleStegaNet employs a four-stage pipeline: (1) stego generation, (2) stylized transmission, (3) structure-preserving reconstruction, and (4) secret recovery. The method uses a progressive three-stage training strategy with wavelet-domain constraints and perceptual supervision to guide recoverable information toward structural representations. The key insight is that secret recoverability is restricted to the normalized structural subspace.

Result: Experiments on DIV2K and MS-COCO datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of StyleStegaNet. Few-shot image steganalysis with two deep detectors shows detection accuracy near random guessing (~51%), indicating strong resistance to steganalysis attacks.

Conclusion: StyleStegaNet successfully redefines steganography invisibility through style-concealment transmission. By decoupling the task into four coordinated stages and using progressive training with wavelet-domain constraints, the method achieves robust secret recovery while maintaining extremely low detection rates (~51% accuracy, near random guessing).

Abstract: Image steganography hides secret message within normal images, with most existing works relying on cover-preserving transmission. However, such a paradigm becomes vulnerable once the original cover is exposed or can be reliably approximated. In this paper, we propose StyleStegaNet, a stylized image hiding framework that replaces cover matching with style-concealment transmission. Instead of transmitting a cover-like stego image, StyleStegaNet generates stylized stego images conditioned on publicly available style references, redefining steganography invisibility from cover-preserving concealment to behavior-level camouflage based on style transformation. Such a setting poses a substantial challenge to reliable secret recovery, since neural stylization can significantly alter the feature statistics exploited by deep hiding methods. To address this challenge, StyleStegaNet decouples the overall task into four coordinated stages: stego generation, stylized transmission, structure-preserving reconstruction, and secret recovery. Moreover, StyleStegaNet is optimized with a progressive three-stage training strategy, in which wavelet-domain constraints and perceptual supervision guide the recoverable information toward structural representations. We further provide an analysis showing that secret recoverability is largely restricted to the normalized structural subspace, offering a mechanistic explanation for why directly stylized baselines fail and why a reconstruction-guided recovery path is necessary. Extensive experiments on DIV2K and MS-COCO datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of StyleStegaNet. And few-shot image steganalysis with two deep detectors further shows detection accuracy near random guessing, approximately 51\%.

</details>


### [13] [WorldCrafter: Consistent Video World Model with Implicit 3D-aware Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24984)
*Wangbo Yu,Kunhao Liu,Wenbo Hu,Shenghai Yuan,Chaoran Feng,Haiyang Zhou,Yukun Huang,Yiran Wang,Wang Zhao,Yingmin Luo,Ying Shan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: WorldCrafter is a video world model that learns a camera-queryable implicit 3D-aware memory to enable consistent long-horizon scene exploration across viewpoints. It uses a memory encoder and pose-conditioned readout module to integrate historical observations into view-specific tokens before denoising.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current video world models struggle to maintain consistency with prior observations over long horizons and across different camera viewpoints during interactive exploration of dynamic environments.

Method: The method combines: (1) a memory encoder and pose-conditioned readout module trained jointly with the video generator to compress multi-view evidence into target view-specific tokens, (2) temporal context integration, and (3) few-step distillation. This enables streaming scene exploration from a single input image or text prompt without explicit depth-based correspondences.

Result: Experiments demonstrate substantial gains in long-horizon consistency and camera-control accuracy while preserving visual quality during minute-scale exploration across both static and dynamic scenes.

Conclusion: WorldCrafter successfully enables interactive 3D scene exploration with consistent viewpoint control over extended periods by learning a camera-queryable implicit 3D-aware memory that integrates historical observations into the video generation process.

Abstract: Video world models enable interactive exploration of dynamic environments, yet struggle to respect prior observations over long horizons and across viewpoints. We present WorldCrafter, a video world model that learns a camera-queryable implicit 3D-aware memory for this purpose. The key insight is to let the requested viewpoint shape how multi-view evidence is compressed into the video generator's limited token budget. Trained jointly with the video generator, a memory encoder and pose-conditioned readout module integrate historical observations into a fixed set of target view-specific tokens before denoising, without explicit depth-based correspondences. By combining this memory with recent temporal context and few-step distillation, WorldCrafter enables streaming scene exploration from a single input image or text prompt. Experiments across static and dynamic scenes show substantial gains in long-horizon consistency and camera-control accuracy while preserving visual quality during minute-scale exploration.

</details>


### [14] [Complementary rPPG-Derived and Lip-Region Frequency Cues for Talking-Face Deepfake Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22284)
*Othmane Harraq,Tamer Aldwairi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper investigates lightweight visual-only methods for detecting talking-face deepfakes, comparing rPPG-derived waveforms and lip-region DCT coefficients across seven generators in Celeb-DF++. Lip-region DCT combined with concat fusion achieves AUC 0.891 in-domain, but transferability varies significantly across generators in leave-one-generator-out evaluation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Talking-face deepfakes are detected unevenly by rPPG-based methods across different generators. The study aims to find lightweight visual cues that can detect deepfakes more consistently across different generation methods.

Method: The authors evaluate two visual cues: (1) rPPG-derived waveforms extracted by RhythmFormer, and (2) lip-region discrete cosine transform (DCT) coefficients. They test these on seven TF methods from Celeb-DF++ under a subject-independent protocol, using both in-domain and leave-one-generator-out evaluation.

Result: In-domain results show lip-region DCT matches or exceeds rPPG-derived 1D ResNet on every method except SadTalker. Concat fusion achieves AUC 0.891 vs 0.824 and 0.827 for unimodal baselines. Under leave-one-generator-out, each cue transfers well to three held-out methods, but IP-LAP is near chance for both. Lip-region DCT outperforms full-face DCT on six of seven methods. Static fusion only partly exploits the complementarity between cues.

Conclusion: Lip-region DCT is a effective lightweight cue for TF deepfake detection, often outperforming rPPG-derived signals. The two cues show complementary transferability across generators, but static fusion (concat) doesn't fully exploit this. The rPPG-derived signal is treated as an empirical cue without claiming cardiac origin. IP-LAP remains difficult to detect with these methods.

Abstract: Talking-face (TF) deepfakes are detected unevenly by rPPG-based methods across generators. We study two lightweight visual-only cues, rPPG-derived waveforms extracted by RhythmFormer and lip-region discrete cosine transform (DCT) coefficients, on the seven TF methods of Celeb-DF++ under a subject-independent protocol. In-domain, lip-region DCT matches or exceeds the rPPG-derived 1D ResNet on every method except SadTalker, and Concat fusion reaches AUC 0.891 against 0.824 and 0.827 for the unimodal baselines. Under leave-one-generator-out evaluation the cues split: each transfers clearly better to three held-out methods, and IP-LAP is near chance for both. Concat averages 0.798 but falls below rPPG alone where DCT transfers poorly, so static fusion only partly exploits this complementarity. Lip-region DCT outperforms full-face DCT on six of seven methods. We treat the rPPG-derived signal as an empirical cue and do not claim it is cardiac in origin.

</details>


### [15] [MixiMotion: One-Step Text-to-Motion Generation via Asymmetric Set Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23010)
*Hung Dinh,Binh Mai,Tran Quoc Bao Le,Lam Nguyen,Cong Tran*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MixiMotion is a one-step text-to-motion generation framework that achieves near teacher-level quality (0.835 vs 0.858 semantic alignment) with 89.2x speedup (9.30ms vs 829.58ms latency) by using offline set distillation with asymmetric bidirectional matching and decoded-space kinematic supervision.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Iterative text-to-motion generation methods deliver high-quality and semantically aligned motions but require multiple network evaluations, resulting in substantial inference latency that limits practical applications.

Method: MixiMotion uses offline set distillation with three key components: (1) constructs an offline bank of multiple teacher motions per text prompt; (2) employs asymmetric bidirectional matching to align teacher and student sample sets - teacher-to-student promotes coverage of diverse motions while student-to-teacher suppresses unsupported generations; (3) introduces differentiable decoded-space kinematic supervision to complement normalized representation matching with constraints in the decoded motion space.

Result: On ViMoGen, MixiMotion achieves a semantic alignment score of 0.835, outperforming evaluated one-step baselines and approaching the 0.858 score of its 50-step HY-Motion-1.0-Lite teacher. In blinded human evaluation, MixiMotion obtains 4.33 overall rating compared with 4.50 for the teacher. Generation latency is reduced from 829.58ms to 9.30ms, corresponding to an 89.2x speedup.

Conclusion: MixiMotion demonstrates an effective quality-efficiency trade-off for strict one-step text-to-motion generation, achieving near teacher-level quality with single network evaluation while eliminating teacher queries, iterative sampling, and candidate ranking.

Abstract: Iterative text-to-motion generation delivers high-quality and semantically aligned motions but requires multiple network evaluations, resulting in substantial inference latency. We present \textbf{MixiMotion}, a strict one-step text-to-motion generation framework based on offline set distillation. Instead of distilling a single teacher trajectory for each text prompt, MixiMotion constructs an offline bank of multiple teacher motions and aligns teacher and student sample sets through \textbf{asymmetric bidirectional matching}. The teacher-to-student direction promotes coverage of diverse teacher-supported motions, while the student-to-teacher direction suppresses unsupported generations. We further introduce differentiable decoded-space kinematic supervision to complement normalized representation matching with constraints in the decoded motion space. At inference, MixiMotion generates a complete motion sequence with a single network evaluation, without teacher queries, iterative sampling, or candidate ranking. On ViMoGen, MixiMotion achieves a semantic alignment score of $0.835$, outperforming the evaluated one-step baselines and approaching the $0.858$ score of its 50-step HY-Motion-1.0-Lite teacher. In blinded human evaluation, MixiMotion obtains an overall rating of $4.33$, compared with $4.50$ for the teacher, while outperforming the evaluated one-/few-step baselines. Meanwhile, generation latency is reduced from $829.58$\,ms to $9.30$\,ms, corresponding to an $89.2\times$ speedup. These results demonstrate an effective quality--efficiency trade-off for strict one-step text-to-motion generation.

</details>


### [16] [Beyond the Survey: A Systematic Empirical Study of Detection and Association in Visual MOT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22291)
*Linh Van Ma,Juhua Hu,Wei Cheng,Unse Fatima,Moongu Jeon*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Summary generation failed


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivation analysis unavailable

Method: Method extraction failed

Result: Result analysis unavailable

Conclusion: Conclusion extraction failed

Abstract: This paper presents a comprehensive experimental evaluation and detailed analysis of state-of-the-art multi-object tracking algorithms, with an emphasis on quantifying the individual contributions of detection and association components to overall tracking performance. Unlike existing surveys that primarily offer theoretical categorizations or taxonomies of tracking methods, our work adopts a rigorous experimental perspective grounded in publicly available implementations, providing practical guidance for researchers and practitioners in method selection and system design. We introduce a unified pipeline diagram that consolidates the core components across the two main branches of visual multi-object tracking: tracking-by-detection and end-to-end deep learning paradigms, and systematically analyze the object detection, feature extraction, and data association modules. Through extensive empirical studies on standard benchmarks, including MOT16, MOT17, MOT20, SportsMOT, DanceTrack, and CrowdTrack datasets, we reveal critical insights: (1) detection quality dominates association strategy performance, with detector improvements yielding more than 10% gains compared to less than 5% from refined association strategies; (2) modern deep learning detectors paired with specialized re-identification models significantly outperform joint detection and embedding approaches; and (3) transformer-based end-to-end methods exhibit greater robustness to detection quality variations but at a substantial computational cost. Our findings from extensive experiments provide key insights into component-level effects in MOT, particularly the dominant influence of detection quality relative to association, while offering practical insights for designing and optimizing MOT systems under varying performance and robustness requirements. Code and experimental setups are available at github.com/linh-gist/VisualMOT.

</details>


### [17] [MM-ContextFold: Context Folding for Multimodal Agentic Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23121)
*Yang Tian,Fan Liu,Jingyuan Zhang,Zhenyang Li,Yupeng Hu,Liqiang Nie*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A new framework MM-ContextFold addresses the context explosion problem in multimodal agentic retrieval by using a hierarchical context strategy: maintaining a persistent text-only main context while spawning ephemeral branch contexts for image-dependent tasks, loading images only when needed and discarding them after use.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The context explosion problem in Multimodal Agentic Retrieval (MAR) systems when using frameworks like ReAct, where raw images accumulate in the context and become redundant as they are textualized through external tools, leading to degraded performance and increased output entropy.

Method: Empirical study of ~10,000 trajectories to analyze image redundancy, followed by proposing MM-ContextFold - a training-free framework that maintains a persistent text-only main context for high-level planning and spawns ephemeral branch contexts for image-dependent subtasks. Images are loaded only when needed, results are folded back as textual summaries, and images/branch traces are discarded after completion.

Result: MM-ContextFold improves average accuracy by 6.3 percentage points over ReAct while reducing working context length by 27.5% across seven MAR benchmarks and five backbone models.

Conclusion: The empirical findings demonstrate that raw images become increasingly redundant as they are textualized, and their continued retention harms task accuracy. MM-ContextFold provides an effective training-free solution that significantly improves accuracy while substantially reducing context length, offering a practical approach to managing token-intensive visual content in MAR systems.

Abstract: Multimodal Agentic Retrieval (MAR) requires agents to solve complex information-seeking tasks by iteratively invoking external tools. Typical frameworks such as ReAct maintain raw multimodal inputs and the accumulating interaction history in a single, ever-growing context, leading to the context explosion problem. While existing methods alleviate this issue by compressing redundant text, effective strategies for managing token-intensive visual content remain largely underexplored. To address this gap, we first conduct a systematic empirical study of approximately 10,000 trajectories. The results show that as visual cues are progressively extracted through external tools and textualized into the context, raw images become increasingly redundant. Continued image retention is associated with higher output entropy and can even degrade task accuracy. Motivated by these findings, we propose MM-ContextFold, a training-free framework that loads raw images only when needed. It maintains a persistent, text-only main context for high-level planning and spawns ephemeral branch contexts for image-dependent subtasks. Within each branch, the agent loads the relevant images, completes the subtask, and folds the result back into the main context as a concise textual summary; the images and branch trace are then discarded. Experiments on seven MAR benchmarks across five backbone models show that MM-ContextFold improves average accuracy by 6.3 percentage points over ReAct while reducing the working context length by 27.5\%.

</details>


### [18] [Validating, Not Sampling: Region-Level Robustness of Vision-Language and Vision-Language-Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22293)
*Bogdan Aron,Christopher Brix,Benedikt Brückner,Yanghao Zhang,Panagiotis Kouvaros,Alessio Lomuscio*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper presents the first robustness validation framework (H²V-M) for testing Vision-Language Models (VLMs) and Vision-Language-Action Models (VLAs) across entire continuous regions of photometric and geometric image perturbations, rather than just sampling points. The study evaluates 6 VLMs and 5 VLAs against brightness shifts, camera rotations, and their compositions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing benchmarks for VLMs and VLAs only sample perturbations, which cannot guarantee the absence of failures in untested regions of the perturbation space. Small perturbations to camera images in real-world deployments can lead to significant decision changes.

Method: The authors extend the H²V validation framework to introduce H²V-M, a margin-aware convergence rule that enables validation at the 32B parameter scale. They validate 6 VLMs (from Gemma, InternVL, LLaVA, and Qwen families) and 5 VLAs (from GR00T, OpenVLA, and π families) over brightness shifts, camera rotations, and their compositions.

Result: H²V-M outperforms H²V by an order of magnitude in model queries and finds counterexamples faster than random sampling while providing soundness guarantees. Robustness depends primarily on perturbation type rather than model. VLMs are more robust to large camera rotations than VLAs. Even small perturbations (±1°) can change VLA commanded actions. Robustness depends more on model family than on model size.

Conclusion: The study reveals that VLA robustness is a significant concern, with very small perturbations capable of altering actions. The proposed H²V-M method provides an efficient and sound approach for robustness validation. Model family is a stronger predictor of robustness than model size.

Abstract: Vision-language models (VLMs) and vision-language-action models (VLAs) are increasingly deployed in real-world applications. There, a small perturbation to the recorded camera image may change a decision significantly. However, existing benchmarks for these models only sample perturbations, which does not guarantee the absence of a failure in the untested region. We present the first robustness validation of six VLMs (drawn from the Gemma, InternVL, LLaVA, and Qwen families) and five VLAs (drawn from the GR00T, OpenVLA, and $π$ families) over entire continuous regions of photometric and geometric image perturbation: brightness shifts, camera rotations, and their composition. To this end, we build on the validation framework H$^2$V and introduce H$^2$V-M, a margin-aware convergence rule that makes validation affordable at the 32B parameter scale. We demonstrate that H$^2$V-M outperforms H$^2$V by an order of magnitude in model queries and that it finds counterexamples faster than random sampling while providing soundness guarantees. Our VLM and VLA robustness validation shows that robustness is mostly dependent on the perturbation type, rather than the model, and that VLMs are more robust to large camera rotations than VLAs. For VLAs, even perturbations as small as $\pm1^\circ$ can change the commanded action in many cases. We also show that robustness depends more on model family than on model size.

</details>


### [19] [GeoBalance: Geometry-Aware Monitoring and Reconstruction with Asymmetric Optimization for Balanced Multimodal Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23533)
*Zechang Xiong,Da Li,Rong Yin,Kexin Tang,Biao Yang,Pengyuan Li,Wenkang Kong,Yulan Hu,Shengyu Zhu,Hao Peng*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper identifies a new problem in multimodal learning called "manifold modality collapse" (MMC), where weak modality representations collapse onto fewer directions within classes and become less separable across classes. The authors propose GeoBalance, a geometry-aware framework that monitors geometric properties and reconstructs weak modality representations using Simplex-ETF class scaffolds and spectral regularization when MMC is detected.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing balancing methods treat modality imbalance as an optimization problem, assuming weak modality representations are intact. However, the authors discover that persistent modality dominance can cause representation-level collapse of weak modalities, which existing methods fail to address.

Method: GeoBalance monitors two geometric properties (class-wise direction collapse and cross-class separability). When MMC is detected, it uses a fixed Simplex-ETF class scaffold with spectral regularization to restore class separation and prevent collapse. Asymmetric gradient projection removes conflicting joint-gradient components while preserving non-conflicting optimization.

Result: Extensive experiments across six multimodal benchmarks demonstrate significant improvements over competitive balancing methods.

Conclusion: The paper successfully identifies and addresses manifold modality collapse (MMC) in multimodal learning through a geometry-aware approach that reconstructs weak modality representations when they exhibit signs of collapse, achieving state-of-the-art performance on multiple benchmarks.

Abstract: Multimodal classifiers can converge to modality-dominant solutions in which one modality dominates the joint prediction, suppressing the learning of others. Existing balancing methods mainly adjust losses, gradients, or modality contributions, largely treating modality imbalance as an optimization problem while implicitly treating the weak modality as under-optimized but representationally intact. In this work, we find that this assumption does not always hold, as persistent modality dominance can induce a representation-level collapse of the weak modality, which we term \emph{manifold modality collapse} (MMC). MMC manifests as a coupled geometric degradation in which weak-modality representations collapse onto fewer directions within each class and become less separable across classes. Motivated by this observation, we propose \emph{GeoBalance}, a geometry-aware framework that monitors these two geometric properties and reconstructs the weak modality representation only when it exhibits signs of MMC. Once triggered, GeoBalance uses a fixed Simplex-ETF class scaffold and spectral regularization to restore class separation while preventing collapse onto a few feature directions. To preserve reconstruction during joint training, asymmetric gradient projection removes the joint-gradient component conflicting with reconstruction, leaving non-conflicting optimization unchanged. Extensive experiments across six multimodal benchmarks demonstrate great improvements over competitive balancing methods, validating its effectiveness.

</details>


### [20] [On The Robustness-Resolution Tradeoff In Temporal Quantization Of Event Streams](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22295)
*Sayeed Shafayet Chowdhury,Ruhi Sharmin*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper investigates the stability issue of timestamp discretization in event-based pipelines (e.g., event cameras, spiking neural networks). The authors prove that hard temporal binning is discontinuous and that any continuous, mass-preserving encoder has a minimum L1 sensitivity of 2/Δ. Linear two-bin interpolation achieves this lower bound, providing optimal stability while maintaining accuracy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Event pipelines often discretize asynchronous timestamps before learning, but this discretization step's stability directly depends on temporal resolution. The authors aim to understand and resolve the instability issues caused by temporal binning at different resolutions.

Method: The authors (1) prove that hard temporal binning is discontinuous (small timestamp shifts near boundaries can move event mass between bins); (2) define a class of nonnegative, mass-preserving, resolution-faithful continuous encoders; (3) prove that every encoder in this class has global L1 sensitivity at least 2/Δ (Δ = bin width); (4) show that linear two-bin interpolation attains this limit with local support and first-moment preservation properties.

Result: Linear interpolation lowers mean representation drift by 47-72% across uniform timestamp budgets while maintaining nearly unchanged accuracy. On DVS Gesture, it produces zero prediction flips across all tested budgets and three seeds. On SHD, measured drift follows 1/Δ with R² = 0.992.

Conclusion: Linear two-bin interpolation provides an optimal, resolution-faithful encoding scheme that achieves the theoretical lower bound for L1 sensitivity, significantly improving representation stability without sacrificing accuracy. This work provides a principled approach to timestamp discretization in event-based learning.

Abstract: Event pipelines often discretize asynchronous timestamps before learning. This step looks harmless, but its stability depends directly on temporal resolution. We study this dependence at the representation level. We first show that hard temporal binning is discontinuous: an arbitrarily small timestamp shift near a boundary can move unit event mass between bins. We then define a class of nonnegative, mass-preserving, resolution-faithful continuous encoders and prove that every encoder in this class has global L1 sensitivity at least 2/Delta, where Delta denotes bin width. Linear two-bin interpolation attains this limit. Local support and first-moment preservation also make it unique. Experiments on SHD, N-MNIST, and DVS128 Gesture support the analysis. Across uniform timestamp budgets, linear interpolation lowers mean representation drift by 47-72% while keeping clean accuracy nearly unchanged. On DVS Gesture, it produces zero prediction flips across all tested budgets and three seeds. On SHD, measured drift follows 1/Delta with R^2 = 0.992.

</details>


### [21] [Authority-Preserving Evaluation of Medical Vision-Language Assistants](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22302)
*Flint Xiaofeng Fan,Cheston Tan,Yew-Soon Ong,Roger Wattenhofer*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: AuthEval is a framework for evaluating the "authority gap" in medical vision-language models - the difference between what a model proposes for skin lesion triage and what local services actually do. It addresses the problem that model performance metrics don't transfer directly to real-world workflow outcomes because local review can modify proposals.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Medical vision-language models can propose urgency scores for skin lesions, but local services retain authority to accept or modify these proposals based on referral policy, capacity, and patient context. This creates a gap between proposal quality and selected-action quality that existing benchmarks fail to capture.

Method: AuthEval logs both proposed and selected actions, scores the selected action under local criteria, and when feasible, scores the declined proposal under the same rules. It reports the authority gap only when the record supports it. The gap is calculated as the product of the proposal-change rate and the mean score change on changed cases. Tested on ISIC 2019 with MedGemma and simulated local review under different constraint regimes (capacity vs safety).

Result: Two constraint regimes with similar change rates produced different gaps: an optimistic +0.744 under capacity constraints but no detectable gap under safety constraints. The evaluation unit also mattered - under mixed constraints, the gap reversed from +0.374 to -0.206 when weighting shifted from image to lesion-aware cluster.

Conclusion: The authority gap cannot be determined by change rate alone; it depends on both change rate AND mean score change. AuthEval clarifies whether study records support claims about the model, the workflow, or both, providing a more accurate evaluation framework for clinical AI deployment.

Abstract: Medical vision-language models can propose how urgently a skin lesion should be reviewed, but the local service retains authority to accept or replace that proposal under referral policy, capacity, and locally held patient context. Proposal quality and selected-action quality are therefore distinct evaluation targets, and benchmark evidence transfers between them only when local review preserves the expected action score. We introduce AuthEval, a logging and evaluation framework that records both actions, scores the selected action under declared local criteria, and, where feasible, scores the declined proposal under the same rule. It reports the resulting authority gap only when the record supports it. Because the gap is the product of the proposal-change rate and the mean score change on changed cases, that rate alone determines neither its magnitude nor its sign. On ISIC 2019, with MedGemma and simulated local review, two constraint regimes with similar change rates produced an optimistic image-equal gap under capacity ($+0.744$ simulator units) but no detectable gap under safety. The declared evaluation unit also mattered: under mixed constraints the gap reversed from $+0.374$ to $-0.206$ when weighting shifted from image to lesion-aware cluster. AuthEval thus clarifies whether a study's records support claims about the model, the workflow, or both.

</details>


### [22] [GameReplica: A Benchmark for Black-Box Visual Game Replication by Vision-Language Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22308)
*Boyu Qiao,Zixin Tang,Xiaoshuai Hao,Wenbo Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: GameReplica is a benchmark for evaluating coding agents' ability to replicate black-box games through visual observation and interaction, without access to source code. It contains 125 tasks across 25 games at 5 difficulty levels. Current agents struggle with this task, with the best model (Claude Opus 4.8) achieving 71.6% and others scoring 4.0%-42.9%.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing coding-agent benchmarks evaluate implementation after specifications are provided. However, it remains underexplored whether agents can induce rules through visual observation and active interaction under black-box conditions (no source code or documentation available) and reproduce the target system as a verifiable executable.

Method: GameReplica is a closed-loop evaluation framework covering perception, exploration, induction, reproduction, and verification. It comprises 125 tasks spanning 25 games across 5 core mechanism families, with each game at five difficulty levels (L1-L5). Agents access games only through screenshots and an action interface, induce visual elements and gameplay rules from pixel feedback, and generate self-contained runnable replicas verified by external programs.

Result: Current coding agents face substantial challenges. Claude Opus 4.8 achieves 71.6% overall score, while others score only 4.0%-42.9%. Visual-fidelity scores are substantially higher than implementation- and rule-consistency scores across all models. Performance gap amplifies with difficulty: weaker agents' scores drop sharply from L1 to L5, while the best-performing agent declines only slightly.

Conclusion: Agents replicate visual appearance more readily than game mechanics. The benchmark reveals a fundamental limitation in current coding agents' ability to induce and implement complex game rules from black-box observation, highlighting the need for improvements in rule induction and implementation consistency.

Abstract: Coding-agent benchmarks usually evaluate implementation after the target behavior has been specified in text, code, or demonstrations. Existing research has extensively evaluated the ability of coding agents to generate programs from textual specifications. However, under black-box conditions where neither source code nor documentation is available, it remains underexplored whether an agent can induce the rules solely through visual observation and active interaction and reproduce the target system as a verifiable executable system. To this end, we present GameReplica, a closed-loop evaluation framework for end-to-end black-box game replication that covers the full perception, exploration, induction, reproduction, and verification pipeline. GameReplica comprises 125 tasks spanning 25 games across 5 core mechanism families, with each game instantiated at five difficulty levels. The tasks require an agent to access the target game only through screenshots and an action interface, induce the key visual elements and gameplay rules from pixel feedback and interaction outcomes, and generate a self-contained, runnable game replica that can be automatically verified by an external program. Experiments show that current coding agents still face substantial challenges in end-to-end black-box replication: the best-performing model (Claude Opus 4.8) achieves an overall score of 71.6\%, while the remaining models score only 4.0\%--42.9\%. Further analysis reveals a consistent pattern across all models: visual-fidelity scores are substantially higher than implementation- and rule-consistency scores, indicating that agents replicate visual appearance more readily than game mechanics. The difficulty levels further amplify the performance gap: from L1 to L5, the overall score of weaker agents drops sharply, whereas that of the best-performing agent declines only slightly.

</details>


### [23] [Yarn tracking of large-scale 3D textile reinforcements using topological material features](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22315)
*Hafsa El Herichi,Arturo Mendoza,Yanneck Wielhorski,Hugues Talbot,Stéphane Roux*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Summary generation failed


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivation analysis unavailable

Method: Method extraction failed

Result: Result analysis unavailable

Conclusion: Conclusion extraction failed

Abstract: Automated segmentation of CT images has become increasingly important to enhance the reliability of simulations through the generation of high fidelity numerical models. This study addresses the challenging task of semi-automatically tracking textile reinforcements in fan blade dry preforms using X-ray CT images captured at coarse resolutions (i.e., above 140 $μ$m). Our approach offers a scalable, slice-based analysis conducted on planes orthogonal to the main yarn directions, applied to a large-scale real industrial component. This enables accurate identification and tracking of yarn paths while requiring minimal training. The method models three key yarn properties statistically: their typical cross-section shape, their continuity and movement in the 3D space, and their spatial relative arrangement with respect to neighboring yarns. These statistical properties are integrated into a tracking framework via a variational formulation that optimizes all yarn center positions in successive cross-section planes. The method tracks more than 3,000 warp yarns across 1,500 slices and achieves a tracking success rate above 90%. Overall, this work demonstrates a promising approach toward large-scale, automated textile reinforcement annotation, paving the way for more efficient material characterization in complex composite structures.

</details>


### [24] [ALPINE: Adaptive Localization for Parameter- and Sample-Efficient Few-Shot Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22323)
*Neeraj Yadav*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper presents an ultra-lightweight (22-35k parameters) spatial-relational architecture for few-shot learning that combines fixed Gabor edge-energy guidance with a content-adaptive patch locator. Under strict iso-episode-budget evaluation, it outperforms Prototypical Networks, Relation Networks, and MAML on CIFAR-FS and MiniImageNet while using 27-53% fewer parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address the limitation that few-shot learning research is predominantly evaluated on accuracy alone, ignoring parameter and training-sample budgets - a real constraint for practitioners without large-scale compute.

Method: 1) Ultra-lightweight spatial-relational architecture (22,249-34,917 parameters) combining fixed Gabor edge-energy guidance with windowed, content-adaptive patch locator; 2) Strictly matched iso-episode-budget protocol (250 meta-training episodes, 5 canonical seeds, 600 evaluation episodes per seed); 3) Falsification ablations testing relational tokens and training without relational components.

Result: 5-shot accuracy gains consistent across all five seeds over all three baselines on CIFAR-FS and MiniImageNet; uses 27-53% fewer parameters; converges in fewer training episodes; generalizes better to unseen fine-grained domain (CUB-200-2011, zero retraining); more robust to 50% occlusion and 25% spatial translation. Surprisingly, ablations reveal the content-adaptive patch locator is the primary driver (not the relational computation).

Conclusion: The architecture achieves superior accuracy with far fewer parameters under strict computational budget matching. The key finding is that the content-adaptive patch locator drives performance, not the relational computation - honestly reported despite being counter-intuitive. A genuine accuracy plateau exists near 22-35k parameters, with full reproducibility via seed-level results and checkpoint hashes.

Abstract: Few-shot learning research is predominantly evaluated on accuracy alone, with limited attention to the parameter and training-sample budgets required to reach that accuracy - a real constraint for practitioners without large-scale compute. We present an ultra-lightweight (22,249-34,917 parameter) spatial-relational architecture for few-shot image classification that combines fixed Gabor edge-energy guidance with a windowed, content-adaptive patch locator. Under a strictly matched, iso-episode-budget protocol (250 meta-training episodes, 5 canonical seeds, 600 evaluation episodes per seed), our architecture achieves 5-shot accuracy gains, consistent across all five seeds, over Prototypical Networks, Relation Networks, and MAML on both CIFAR-FS and MiniImageNet, while using 27-53% fewer parameters than any baseline. It also converges in fewer training episodes, generalizes better to an unseen fine-grained domain (CUB-200-2011 birds, zero retraining), and is more robust to 50% occlusion and 25% spatial translation than all three baselines. A series of falsification ablations - zeroing relational tokens at inference and retraining without them entirely - shows that the architecture's pairwise relational computation, while present, is not the primary driver of its performance; the content-adaptive patch locator is. We report this honestly, together with a capacity sweep showing a genuine accuracy plateau near 22-35k parameters, and release full seed-level results and checkpoint hashes for reproducibility.

</details>

### [26] [Hierarchical Aggregation of Semantic Uncertainty in 3D Scene Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22351)
*Carlos Cueto Zumaya,Iacopo Catalano,Wallace Moreira Bessa,Julio A. Placed*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper presents a framework for estimating semantic uncertainty in open-vocabulary 3D Scene Graphs (3DSGs).
